We achieve balance by grounding every dialogue in evidence-based research and shared development priorities , rather than political or commercial interests . <p> What would you say are ACCPA 's most significant achievements to date in advancing Africa 's voice in Africa-China relations ? <p> ACCPA 's greatest achievement has been positioning Africa 's voice at the center of Africa -- China cooperation through research , dialogue , and policy engagement . @@130312631 <p> The men who cornered Ita James did not come for his catch . In this bustling town of Ibaka , one of Nigeria 's largest fishing towns , fishers know how sudden and brutal such an encounter could be . Speedboats fitted with twin 200-horsepower engines emerge from the horizon , shots are fired , and fishermen are forced by men armed with automatic rifles to promptly unbolt and hand over their outboard engines . Those who resist are beaten , sometimes killed . <p> After the attackers vanish , the victims ' boats are left dead on the water , drifting until help arrives . Many fishers return home broken , having borrowed to replace a stolen engine , only to lose it again . <p> " It is like working for the criminals , " said Mr James , who lost two engines in two years . " It happened more than once ; I lost them . Some people have lost engines three or more times . " <p> After he escaped a third attack , this time with a borrowed motor , the owner promptly @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a hired hand on another boat , earning just enough to keep his family . " <p> During an interview in August , Mr James sat on a wooden bench with three other fishermen at Ibaka 's main harbour , repairing nets at a time when owners of engine-powered boats were at sea . <p> " They take our engines , phones , money ; sometimes your fish and everything , " said Moses Lawrence , another fisher . " And you will be stranded and , if God helps you , another boat comes around . It is very common here ; there is almost nobody who has not suffered this problem . " <p> He speaks flatly , his face lean and weathered in the morning sun , as the Atlantic lapped the coastline and dozens of traders walked by . <p> Officially , Nigeria 's waters are now safer . In fact , water is often the first and most visible victim of climate change , " he said . <p> " In 2010 , the United Nations General Assembly ( UNGA ) adopted a historical resolution recognising ' the right to safe and clean drinking water and sanitation as a human right that is essential for the full enjoyment of life and all human rights ' . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a twin problem of both government failure and nature 's response to man 's devastating use of the environment . <p> Advertisement <p> " At a time when we need stronger , publicly accountable systems to guarantee universal access , we are seeing growing pressure to privatise our water systems and hand control of this essential resource to profit-driven corporations , despite overwhelming public opposition . <p> " While nature cries for justice in the face of the climate emergency , capital smells opportunity . @@130350619 <p> Nigeria has launched the net zero nature-positive project ( NZNP ) , a national initiative designed to tackle climate change , restore degraded ecosystems , and accelerate the country 's transition to a low-carbon economy by 2060 . <p> The project was inaugurated in Abuja during an inception workshop on Monday . <p> Halima Bawa-Bwari , director of forestry at the federal ministry of environment , described the NZNP as " a pivotal moment in Nigeria 's collective journey toward a climate-resilient and inclusive future " . <p> She said the initiative would strengthen institutional coordination , enhance biodiversity , and attract investments that prioritise nature-positive and low-carbon growth . <p> Advertisement <p> " Today marks a pivotal moment in our collective journey toward a future that is not only climate-resilient but also rich in biodiversity and inclusive in its development , " Bawa-Bwari said . <p> " Nigeria 's commitment to a net-zero , nature-positive future is not just aspirational , it is grounded in action from updating our newly submitted Nationally Determined Contributions ( NDCs ) . " <p> Bawa-Bwari said the NDCs is part of efforts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ adaptation benefits . <p> Advertisement <p> She added that the project offers a " transformative opportunity " to integrate emissions reduction with environmental restoration and community development . <p> Elsie Attafuah , resident representative of the United Nations Development Programme ( UNDP ) in Nigeria , said the NZNP reflects Nigeria 's " bold and necessary commitment " to achieve net-zero emissions by 2060 . <p> Attafuah said the project aims to conserve 30 percent of Nigeria 's land and marine ecosystems by 2030 , particularly focusing on vulnerable regions like the Niger Delta . <p> She noted that climate change is disrupting lives and livelihoods , as well as accelerating biodiversity loss . <p> Advertisement <p> " Through this project , we will work with state governments in Rivers and Bayelsa to integrate net-zero and nature-positive strategies into development planning , " she said . <p> " We will partner with women 's cooperatives and youth networks , ensuring they are not only beneficiaries but leaders of the green transition . " <p> The NZNP is funded by the Global Environment Facility ( GEF ) and jointly implemented by @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ United Nations Environment Programme ( UNEP ) . <p> Nkechi Aneke-Agnes , director of planning , research and statistics at the environment ministry , said the NZNP accelerator is part of the 11 integrated programmes under the GEF-8 cycle , designed to scale up investments in nature-based and technological climate solutions . <p> Advertisement <p> She noted that the initiative represents " a significant step " in Nigeria 's path to achieving its net-zero and nature-positive targets , expanding access to clean energy , and de-risking sustainable investments . <p> Government officials from Rivers and Bayelsa state ministries of environment also pledged their support , noting the project 's importance for regional biodiversity conservation . 
@@130350620 <h> The Nigerian paradox : A nation that blames itself , not its immigrants <p> In an era where migrants are blamed for everything from job losses to cultural decline , from Brexit Britain to Trump 's America and South African Xenophobia , Nigeria stands out for a curious paradox . That paradox is how Nigerians rarely target the foreigners who live and work in the country , despite the challenges that the people face . <p> Across the globe , anti-immigrant sentiment has become a political force . In the United States , Donald Trump 's 2016 " build-the-wall " campaign turned immigration into a defining wedge issue ; surveys showed that well over half of his voters listed immigration as a " critical threat " . That same sentiment drove his return to the White House in 2025 . <p> The United Kingdom 's 2016 Brexit referendum , a narrow 52% to 48% vote by the people to leave the EU , was fuelled by fears that refugees and Eastern Europeans were " taking jobs " , putting pressure on social services and changing local communities . Also @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is often weaponised in local politics in Spain and The Netherlands . <p> In Africa , waves of Xenophobic attacks in South Africa between 2008 and 2019 left several people dead ; mostly Zimbabweans , Malawians and Nigerians . Kenya was not left out as protests against Chinese contractors erupted , following accusations of sharp labour practices . <p> Advertisement <p> We Keep It in the Family <p> Nigeria has its own crises : high unemployment , overstretched cities , deep inequality and long-running insecurity . Yet , unlike many countries under similar pressure , Nigerians seldom vent their frustration on the foreigners within their borders . <p> Our fiercest quarrels rarely cross the border . We reserve the sharpest arrows for one another , in WhatsApp groups , on radio call-in shows , in partisan shouting matches , in endless tribal banter that can turn bitter . Even when we take jabs at other countries , Nigerians will tell you that it is never without provocation , that we only clap back when someone else throws the first punch . <p> Too often , our sense of national unity @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ than a bond we all live by . Yet , remarkably , we almost never go after the foreigners who have long lived , worked and prospered among us . <p> A Lesson Learned the Hard Way <p> To be clear , Nigeria is no sanctuary and Nigerians are no saints . History reminds us that we once scapegoated outsiders , and paid for it . <p> Advertisement <p> In 1983 , amid an oil-price slump and economic crisis , the Shehu Shagari Government ordered the mass expulsion of more than a million West African migrants , mostly Ghanaians , blaming them for rising unemployment . Migrants fled in haste , carrying their belongings in the red-blue chequered plastic bags that then became known as " Ghana-Must-Go " bags . Another round of expulsion followed in 1984 unde the Muhamadu Buhari Military Government . These deportations left deep scars on Nigeria 's reputation and on relations with neighbours for decades . <p> Since that fiasco , Nigeria has largely avoided large-scale expulsions or anti-immigrant street violence . <p> Still , Nigeria 's relative tolerance is not without tension . There @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ some Chinese-run mines in mineral-rich states allegedly mistreating local workers or using exploitative contracts , and from time to time , there are complaints about certain Lebanese-owned factories over labour conditions or local-sourcing practices . These murmurs may trend briefly on social media . But they rarely translate into mass street movements or political slogans . <p> A Century of Shared Hustle <p> Advertisement <p> Foreigners are not outsiders to Nigeria 's economy ; they are woven into it . <p> The Lebanese merchants who arrived in significant numbers in the 1920s and built enduring family businesses in textiles , construction and retail in Lagos and Kano . The Indians who expanded into trade after independence and still dominate segments of pharmaceuticals , plastics and light manufacturing . The Chinese , virtually absent 25 years ago , now Nigeria 's single largest import partner and key investors in railways , power and highways . Ghanaians , Kenyans and South Africans hold important posts in Nigerian banks , consultancies and telecoms . Liberians and Sierra Leoneans , who fled the 1990s civil wars , have integrated into Nigerian cities . The @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ often carving out comfortable niches . <p> Advertisement <p> Nigerians notice who controls which sector ; we may grumble about unfair competition or poor labour conditions , but we do not push for them to exit . In 2022 , Nigeria 's main import partners were China , Netherlands , Belgium , India and the U.S. ; top export destinations included India , Spain , Netherlands , Indonesia and the U.S. <p> In search for who to blame , Nigerians often look to their governments for their mismanagement and corruption . They do not focus their bile on the foreign individuals building businesses . <p> These are not just sayings ; they are social codes that have survived urbanisation and modern stress . Nigerians may quarrel bitterly among themselves , but a cultural duty of hospitality tempers how we treat outsiders . <p> Equally powerful is a national admiration for hustle . One unspoken rule cuts across class and ethnicity : " If you hustle and succeed , we respect you . " Whether you 're an Igbo electronics trader in Alaba , a Yoruba textile seller in Balogun , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the grind . <p> The Political Lens : Anger Turned Inward <p> Nigeria 's politics has conditioned the nation to point fingers inward . Leaders , rather than migrants , are seen as the architects of dysfunction . On radio call-ins , TV talk shows and especially on X/Twitter , the chorus is constant : " Our government failed us " ; " Our leaders betrayed us . " <p> Even during the xenophobic attacks on Nigerians in South Africa , reprisal protests at home focused on symbols of South African corporate power in Nigeria -- Shoprite supermarkets and MTN offices . South Africans citizens in Lagos and Abuja were largely left alone . In fact , greater anger was directed at the Nigerian Government 's failure to protect its citizens abroad . <p> A Rare Paradox Worth Protecting <p> In an era when climate change , conflicts and economic shocks will continue to drive unprecedented migration , Nigerians ' instinct to direct frustration inward rather than at outsiders is a paradox worth protecting . <p> This resilience is no accident . It grows out of deep-rooted traditions of hospitality @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ holding our own leaders , not immigrants , to account . Together , these codes have spared us the divisive anti-immigrant populism destabilising other nations . <p> That spirit is a national asset . With fairer policies , stronger labour protections , and more inclusive politics , Nigeria can remain a place where migrants and investors alike find opportunity and where grievances stay in the family . <p> In a fractured world , this under-recognised strength and quiet lesson in tolerance are resources that we would do well to nurture with care . <p> Oma writes from Madrid , Spain , on Leadership , Culture and Change . <p> Views expressed by contributors are strictly personal and not of TheCable. 
@@130350635 <h> Nigeria , Africa , and the uneven burden of climate change <p> In October 2022 , devastating floods displaced over 1.4 million Nigerians , destroyed hundreds of thousands of hectares of farmland , and claimed more than 600 lives . These floods , one of the worst in decades , were not an isolated incident . Across Africa , droughts , heatwaves , coastal erosion , and other climate-related disasters continue to destroy livelihoods . Yet the continent contributes less than 4% of global greenhouse gas emissions . <p> This is the paradox at the heart of the climate crisis : Africa , already struggling with poverty and underdevelopment , is among the regions worst affected by climate change but bears little responsibility for it . Nigeria , the continent 's largest economy and most populous country , contributes roughly 1% of global emissions . By contrast , China accounts for about 30% , the United States 14% , and the European Union 8% . <p> The data is clear , but the international debate is not . Should Africa be expected to bear the same burden in the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ most to it ? <p> Advertisement <p> This article was primarily prompted by a conversation I had with an old colleague who works at a multinational Non-Governmental Organization ( NGO ) focused on promoting Sustainable Development Goals ( SDGs ) . We spoke about a number of things , and while we did agree on a number of those things , we could n't find a common ground when it came to this issue in particular , and I would like to present my line of thought in this article . <p> The principle of " common but differentiated responsibilities , " enshrined in international climate agreements , recognises that while all nations must act , the weight of action should fall more heavily on those who bear the greatest historical responsibility . Developed countries industrialised long before Africa , reaping enormous economic benefits from fossil fuels in the process . <p> Yet in today 's global climate discourse , this principle is often forgotten . Wealthy nations are setting ambitious emission reduction targets and encouraging others to do the same , often without acknowledging the vast differences in capacity @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ' at All Costs <p> The idea that Africa should match the pace and scale of emission cuts in industrialised nations is unrealistic and unfair . More than 56% of Nigerians live below the poverty line . Our economy still depends heavily on oil and gas , not just for energy , but also for the revenue that sustains healthcare , education , and infrastructure . <p> Advertisement <p> To shut down fossil fuel use abruptly without affordable alternatives would cripple economic growth , worsen poverty , and deny millions access to reliable electricity . Developed countries can afford to accelerate their transitions because they already have strong economies , advanced technologies , and social safety nets . Africa does not . <p> World leaders in Washington , Brussels , and Beijing can announce massive cuts in their carbon emissions because they have the resources , technology , and infrastructure to back up their ambitions . African countries are still fighting for basic electricity access in homes and hospitals . <p> We can not afford to be punished for the sins of others . That is why Nigeria and other @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ We must insist that developed nations stop imposing conditionalities on aid and loans that require unrealistic energy transitions . <p> Instead , they should work with us to build renewable energy systems , strengthen our economies , and help us prepare for a future where the effects of climate change are already locked in . <p> Advertisement <p> Fossil fuels are not forever , and truly , Nigeria must diversify its economy and develop greener energy systems . But we can not do so at a pace that undermines development . <p> Cutting emissions too aggressively now , without viable alternatives , would worsen poverty and inequality . Millions of Nigerians already live without consistent access to electricity . For them , the priority is not decarbonisation targets but survival . <p> Advertisement <p> It is important to me that this is not seen as an argument for inaction . It is a call for climate justice : a fair approach that recognises our different starting points . <p> What Africa Should Prioritise <p> Advertisement <p> Africa must take climate change seriously , but our focus should be tailored to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ afford to ignore : <p> Sustainable Development : Africa 's economies need to grow , but growth must not come at the expense of our natural resources . We must invest in industries that create jobs and lift people out of poverty while preserving our environment . <p> Climate Finance : Developed nations must honour their commitments to fund adaptation and mitigation efforts in vulnerable countries . They bear the historical responsibility for emissions and must support Africa with financing and technology . <p> Local Solutions : Renewable energy sources like solar and wind are well-suited to Africa 's geography and could bridge the energy access gap in rural communities . But these investments must be scaled up significantly . <p> Climate Justice for Africa <p> Climate change is a global crisis that demands global solutions . But those solutions must be rooted in justice . Africa is already paying a heavy price despite its minimal role in creating the problem . <p> The world must recognise that the fight against climate change can not succeed without fairness . Developed nations , who have enjoyed the benefits of industrialisation , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ finance , technology , and support that vulnerable nations like Nigeria need to adapt and grow sustainably . <p> We must chart a balanced path : meeting our current energy needs while laying the foundation for a cleaner , more sustainable future . This means investing in renewable energy , building robust infrastructure , and diversifying our economies away from oil dependency . But it must also mean refusing to accept unrealistic expectations that could hold us back . <p> Africa deserves partnership , not blame . Climate action must uplift , not undermine , our development . If the world is serious about solving this crisis , it must start by ensuring that the most affected regions are not asked to sacrifice the very progress they are struggling to achieve .

Stunting ( low height for age ) is caused by long-term nutritional deprivation . It affects brain development , school performance , and future productivity , while wasting ( low weight for height ) signals recent and severe weight loss , often from acute food shortage or disease , and poses a high risk of death . <p> Children who are malnourished have weakened immunity , leaving them prone to frequent infections such as diarrhoea , pneumonia , and malaria . A simple childhood illness can become fatal when compounded by poor nutrition . <p> Data from the WHOshows that globally in 2022 , 149 million children under five were estimated to be stunted , 45 million were estimated to be wasted , and 37 million were overweight or living with obesity . <p> The WHO said nearly half of deaths among children under five years of age are linked to undernutrition , most of which occur in low- and middle-income countries . <p> According to the 2023 Nigeria Demographic and Health Survey , Nigeria has @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the country with the highest burden in Africa and the second highest in the world . The report shows that Kano State has 1.5 million children under five years of age who are stunted . <p> Doctors warn that children who survive early malnutrition often face lifelong consequences , including poor cognitive development , delayed growth , learning difficulties , and increased risk of chronic diseases in adulthood . <h> One of many others <p> Umar 's case is not isolated . It said the COVID-19 pandemic , soaring inflation , and the Russia-Ukraine war have all contributed to surging food and energy prices between 2020 and 2024 , further deepening the food access crisis . <p> &gt; " As food prices rose , real wages fell . Inflation left people -- especially in low-income countries -- unable to afford nutritious food , often forcing families to reduce their meals to just one per day , " the WFP stated . <p> Beyond economics , social inequality is another factor . @@130428845 <h> What NLC wants from COP30 and how to get them <p> The upcoming United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change 's thirtieth Conference of the Parties , popularly known as COP30 , to take place in Bel ? m , Brazil , in November , has been declared by the Brazilian Presidency as the " Implementation COP " , meaning the focus is shifting from negotiation of new goals to delivering on what has already been agreed under the Paris Agreement and other climate frameworks . Therefore , considering that workers are most impacted by climate change while carbon emissions generally take place in the workplace , COP30 shall revolve around the workers , who are demanding a Just Transition to a green future . <p> The Action Agenda is central : a structure for mobilising all actors -- national governments , sub-national , private sector , civil society , investors , among others -- to amplify , scale , and track climate solutions . But specifically for workers , the Bel ? m Action Mechanism for a Global Just Transition is a proposed new institutional arrangement under the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Global Just Transition efforts . <p> Remarkably , one thing that has prepared Nigeria for the emerging COP dynamic is the recently validated climate action framework known as the Just Transition Guidelines and Action Plan , JT-GAP for short , which has brought Nigeria immense respect in the comity of nations and would steer the nation 's shift to a green and climate-resilient economy . It also clearly demonstrated that the National Council on Climate Change incorporates the Nigerian workers ' concerns at the centre of Nigeria 's response to climate change . <p> The second element that sets the path to COP30 for the Nigerian worker is the ongoing NLC pre-COP30 Affiliate Unions Climate Change Focal Persons Retreat and Regional Strategy Visioning Meeting . Led by the National Coordinator of the NLC Climate Change , Green Jobs and Just Transition Programme , Comrade Echezona Asuzu , it is set to translate grassroots labour concerns into a coherent , evidence-based negotiating posture for the conference . Hence , it is important to review the purpose and outputs of the meeting , and other NLC pre- and post-COP engagements , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and the NLC 's influence at COP30 . <p> The meeting and regional strategy review session is multipurpose and practical . It consolidates research , workers ' survey results and previous pre-COP findings into a single set of policy asks and talking points ( e.g. , on NDCs , just transition , social protection and sectoral impacts ) . The NLC has used consolidated research outputs and validation workshops to shape its formal positions in the past , and this is why we are looking at a reinvigorated showing at COP30 . <p> The " regional strategy review " element brings state and zonal union leadership together so demands and narratives are not just Abuja-centric but reflect frontline realities ( agriculture , oil/gas/energy communities , informal sector , health and mining ) . This strengthens NLC 's legitimacy when it speaks for Nigerian workers . <p> Secondly , with the coordination with international trade union bodies , the meeting feeds into broader African and global trade-union coordination ( e.g. , the International Trade Union Confederation , Africa ) , sharpening a common negotiating platform for COP . For instance , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ pre-COP event in Brasilia , Brazil . <p> In consonance with past NLC pre-COP work , which explicitly aimed to galvanise a collective trade-union position at COPs , the pertinent issues are explored in three themes . The first is Just Transition with social protection and green jobs , which in practical terms is a demand for policies that protect livelihoods and scale decent , sustainable green employment . The second is Climate finance and justice , which is a bold demand for major finance flows to the Global South to fund adaptation , loss and damage and worker transitions . It is instructive to note that the NLC has consistently framed huge financial needs in justice terms . The third is evidence-led advocacy , which is pushing empirical , sectoral evidence ( surveys , validation reports ) so the NLC 's asks are concrete and defensible in negotiations . <p> In looking towards COP30 , I believe that NLC 's robust preparedness would galvanise a clearer , worker-centred negotiating posture for Nigeria , which unequivocally has positioned the country as a model for worker-friendly climate action . The NLC @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , making it more likely Nigeria 's delegation will publicly foreground just transition , social protection and the labour impacts of mitigation/adaptation policies , which are in line with the demands of the Global South . <p> Secondly , a stronger coalition and bargaining power . By aligning state/regional union inputs and linking with African trade union positions , the NLC can punch above its weight : coordinated regional labour demands are harder for rich countries and institutions to ignore at the plenary , side events or constituency briefings . In other words , the NLC is now positioned to improve on its milestone achievement in the past two COPs -- 28 and 29 , where it creditably pulled off critically acclaimed side events . <p> Thirdly , sharper advocacy on finance and loss and damage . The NLC 's public framing , including high-value finance calls and the demand for climate justice , will embolden Nigeria 's civil society and possibly some government actors to take bolder stances on financing and compensation issues during COP negotiations and side events . This is because , as this year 's climate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ be at the centre of negotiations as climate action can not come alive without it . <p> There is yet another strategy that Nigerian workers should explore for concentric participation at the COP . For the first time , health is explicitly a priority sector under NDC3.0 for some countries ; so , the Bel ? m Health Plan will explicitly place health and equity at the centre of climate response . <p> Indeed , workers are more vulnerable to the health impacts of climate change than the general population because many work outdoors , in fields such as agriculture , construction , or transportation , exposing them to extremes in temperature and weather , poor air quality , and disease-carrying pests . Even the ones that work indoors , the heat wave has attenuated the amenable ambience , causing their working habitat to become a ' climate hell ' . For instance , the plight of textile workers in North West Nigeria . <p> The Bel ? m Health Action Plan is a chance for Nigeria to bring forward its climate-health linkages ( heat waves , vector diseases , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ position itself as a leader in climate-resilient health systems in Africa . We need to map existing health system vulnerabilities , invest in surveillance , resilience , build capacity , also generate data /evidence , show innovation ( e.g. , mobile health , early warning systems ) and engage vulnerable communities while seeking international support for health adaptation . Surely , the health sector is often underfunded for other competing priorities ( security , economy ) . The NLC can drive this process as they have always done , using their research-based policy briefs and the global COP platforms . <p> This shall speak to the charge of the Acting General Secretary of the NLC , Comrade Benson Upah , to the participants at the NLC pre-COP meeting , when he said , " We must not only rely on the government of Brazil for the results we earnestly desire , but we must also evolve strategies for enhanced collaboration with progressive blocs and parties at COP 30 , including the Group of 77 plus China , the African Group of Negotiators , and non-party stakeholders , such as forward-thinking @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ partners and workplace social actors . " <p> Indubitably , the NLC 's pre-COP synthesis and regional review work is more than a meeting ; it is the architecture that converts local worker experiences into an evidence-based , strategically aligned labour voice at COP30 . If the NLC keeps focus on validated research , coalition building , and resourcing a visible delegation , Nigeria 's presence in Bel ? m can reflect not only national priorities but the rights and needs of workers , increasing the chance that COP30 decisions , side agreements , or finance commitments better account for a just transition in Nigeria . <p> All rights reserved . @@130428949 <h> Hand in hand for better food : Global call to end hunger on World Food Day 2025 <p> The world today marks World Food Day 2025 under the theme " Hand in Hand for Better Foods and a Better Future . " The day , observed every October 16th , highlights the urgent need for collective global action to end hunger and ensure access to nutritious food for all . <p> According to the United Nations and the World Food Programme , global hunger remains a major concern , with over 295 million people across 53 countries facing acute food insecurity in 2024 . Despite slight improvements in some regions , Africa continues to record the highest levels of hunger and malnutrition , driven by conflict , climate change , and economic challenges . <p> In Nigeria , the situation remains critical . Reports from the UN Food and Agriculture Organization , UNICEF , and the World Food Programme indicate that between 30.6 and 33 million Nigerians are facing acute food insecurity in 2025 . This figure reflects a worsening trend from previous years , with conflict , inflation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ The hunger crisis has hit the country 's northern region the hardest , where insecurity has disrupted farming activities and displaced thousands . Humanitarian agencies say children are the most affected , with malnutrition claiming hundreds of lives . In Katsina State alone , over 650 children have died from malnutrition in the first half of 2025 according to Doctors Without Borders <p> The Nigerian federal government says it remains committed to tackling the crisis . Following the state of emergency on food security declared in 2023 , measures have been taken to boost production and stabilize food prices . These include releasing grains and fertilizers from strategic reserves , securing farmlands , and creating a National Commodity Board to regulate food prices . <p> In 2024 , the government intensified its intervention through mechanized farming initiatives covering about 500,000 hectares of land and established the Presidential Food Systems Coordinating Unit to harmonize food policies nationwide . Temporary waivers were also introduced for food imports to cushion the effects of inflation , while funds saved from fuel subsidy removal were redirected to support agriculture and vulnerable households . <p> As @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ from the United Nations remains clear : governments , private sectors , and individuals must work hand in hand to create better food systems -- because a better food future begins with collective action today .

@@130429733 <p> The Africa Family Business Survey by PwC has identified tax reform , succession planning , governance , and sustainability as the principal forces shaping the next chapter of African family businesses . <p> Esiri Agbeyi , partner at PwC Nigeria and Africa Family Business Leader , presented the findings at Business Day 's recently held Family Business Summit , describing it as a pulse check on the current state of family businesses and a practical snapshot of both the challenges they face and the opportunities available to them . <p> Agbeyi noted that the past three years have been characterised by economic volatility , but she observed signs of emerging stability , particularly in foreign exchange markets . <p> Now in its 12th edition , the PwC Family Business Survey is conducted biennially across multiple continents . This year 's dataset includes contributions from 79 African family businesses , and the Africa-specific report complements the global survey findings . <p> The survey also highlights five global megatrends that are shaping the future of family businesses : climate change and sustainability , demographic shifts , social and wealth inequality , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ economic volatility . <p> " For African businesses , economic issues tend to override innovation concerns , " she said . " The insight here is how we can maintain stability without losing focus on what matters : innovation and sustainability . " <p> One of the most significant findings was that succession planning and access to capital remain the biggest challenges for African family enterprises . <p> The survey also found that leadership development and talent retention continue to pose difficulties , especially as younger generations push for modernisation . It also shows that resistance from older generations is beginning to ease . <p> " We are seeing more senior leaders embrace the idea of letting go , " she observed . " The ' how ' remains a challenge , but it is progress . " <p> Access to capital and governance practices <p> African family businesses are largely reinvesting profits to finance growth , rather than relying on external funding . <p> Agbeyi , however , urged enterprises to diversify their sources of capital . <p> " Banks often complain about weak governance structures and incomplete records @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in strategic partnerships , government grants , and subsidies that can be explored . " <p> Governance tools remain underutilised . While wills are common , fewer businesses use shareholder agreements , dividend policies , or family constitutions , which are mechanisms that help preserve long-term objectives . <p> " Not many family businesses realise that these are the instruments that sustain both continuity and stability , " Agbeyi noted . <p> Agbeyi used the coffee value chain as an illustration , explaining that " the highest value is not in the beans but in the service , the retail experience . " She argued that Africa must evolve from resource-based to service-led economies , especially as AI reshapes manufacturing and service delivery . <p> " Traditional business models will change , " she said . " The economies that will thrive are those that can adapt quickly to this shift . " <p> Tax reform and business implications <p> Tax emerged as a defining issue for African family enterprises . While most respondents said they were proud to " pay their fair share of taxes , " they also @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ strategically managed . <p> Agbeyi highlighted several ongoing tax reforms in Nigeria , including changes in the definition of tax residency , capital gains tax ( CGT ) adjustments , and new electronic invoicing rules . <p> " A foreign company can now be considered a Nigerian tax resident , " she explained . " If management and control happen here , it falls under Nigeria 's tax net . That means family businesses using offshore structures need to ensure proper governance and substance . " <p> She advised business owners to stay compliant with the Federal Inland Revenue Service ( FIRS ) e-invoicing system by 2026 , warning that non-compliance could attract fines of up to N200,000 per transaction . <p> " Tax is no longer a cost line ; it is a business issue , " Agbeyi concluded . " We must reframe conversations around how we manage capital , reward family members , and preserve value . " <p> The survey found that African family businesses place a high premium on community impact and legacy preservation . Respondents cited " taking care of their communities " and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ even above financial performance . <p> However , Agbeyi cautioned that intent must be matched with institutional capacity . <p> " While the desire is strong , the structures to make it sustainable are still developing , " she said . <p> She also underscored the growing importance of environmental , social , and governance ( ESG ) considerations . <p> " For some , ESG feels like a soft topic , nice to hear but not nice to do , " she admitted . " But these are now key drivers of capital . " <p> Art , trusts , and the preservation of wealth <p> Closing her presentation , Agbeyi advised on asset management and estate planning , including art as an emerging asset class . <p> " Art is beautiful and valuable , but complex to value , " she remarked . " If you are transferring artwork into trusts or other structures , ensure proper valuation and rebasing . These details matter for compliance and wealth preservation . " <p> Ngozi Ekugo is a Snr . Correspondent/ analyst at Businessday . She has worked across various @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Sachs , London . She holds an MSc Management from the University Hertfordshire , a Bachelor of Arts from the University of Lagos and is an alumna of Queen 's college . She is also an associate member of the Chartered Institute of Personnel Management ( CIPM ) . <p> Business Day , established in 2001 , is a daily business newspaper based in Lagos . @@130468423 <p> One of the nation 's most predominant natural disasters is recurrent flooding , which causes some states to increasingly experience the menace during rainy seasons . BENJAMIN SAMSON in this report examines why floods still hit states despite early warnings by government agencies . <p> 2025 reports <p> According to the National Emergency Management Agency ( NEMA 's ) 2025 Flood Dashboard , no fewer than 236 people have died in floods that have ravaged 27 states and the Federal Capital Territory ( FCT ) so far this year . The report also revealed that 409,714 people have been affected across 117 local government areas , with thousands displaced and extensive destruction done to homes , roads , and farmlands . <p> The report noted further that Niger state recorded the highest number of fatalities with 163 deaths , followed by Adamawa with 59 . Taraba reported five deaths : Sokoto -- three ; Jigawa and Yobe , two each , while Gombe and Borno recorded one death each . <p> Ignored warnings <p> Speaking with this reporter , an Associate Professor of Geography , University of Nigeria , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ have remained a recurring decimal in the nation 's history because Nigerians do n't heed to flood warnings . <p> He said , " Despite efforts by authorities to issue warnings at intervals , many Nigerians have remained unfazed by warnings and the impending disasters . <p> " Water , they say , will always find its own level when people obstruct its path . Such often comes in a hot furry that clears objects , both animate and inanimate , on its way . Such is the case in Nigeria ; every raining season ushers in the menace of flooding , which has become a recurring nightmare for Nigerians for decades , with the country 's geographical location making it prone to severe flooding . <p> " In February , the Nigerian Meteorological Agency ( NiMet ) , in its 2025 Seasonal Climate Prediction ( SCP ) , warned that rainfall would arrive early in parts of the South and late in the North , disrupting the usual rhythm of the wet season . The forecast , designed to guide preparedness across sectors , again proved accurate , but @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Despite repeated forecasts , flooding remains a recurring crisis in Nigeria 's rainy season calendar . Its impact continues to devastate lives , destroy livelihoods , and damage critical infrastructure . The 2012 flood remains the most catastrophic in recent memory , but subsequent years , including 2018 , 2022 , and more recently the 2024 Mokwa floods in Niger state , have served as painful reminders that the threat is far from over . <p> " It may seem that many Nigerians have remained adamant to the warnings over impending flooding in their areas despite the signs that the forecasts are coming to pass . Some others have also said that they were afraid to permanently lose their lands to the government or other private elements if they should move out because of the flood . " <p> Victims narrate ordeals <p> A victim , Mr. Isaiah Odekina , who resides in Onyedega in Ibaji local government area in Kogi state , affected by floods recently , said : " I have been living here for 15 years now , but this is the first time I am experiencing @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that my house and the area would be flooded and not evacuate my family ? " <p> He said the changing patterns of rainfall occasioned by global warming and climate change should be blamed , arguing that the federal and state governments should have made proactive measures to check the menace . <p> Odekina advised the government at all levels to go after those who erect illegal structures that block channels and contribute to flood disasters . <p> " When we built our house in our current location , everywhere was peaceful and there was no incident of flooding . But as the years rolled by , all manner of constructions began to spring up ; some even built on water channels . We reported to the government agencies on several occasions , and they came , but the construction never stopped , and this gave an indication of a compromise . <p> " Today , we are all being affected . If the government had done what it was supposed to do , we would not be lamenting today . You can see how the irresponsibility of some people @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Ojone Shaibu , another resident , said , " This is my father 's land and our ancestral compound ; where do I go to now ? We were not used to this kind of flooding . The first one was in 2012 , after some time it dried up and we came back to our houses because we had nowhere else to evacuate to . There has not been such an incident since then , but it came again this year . " <p> Causes <p> Speaking with this reporter , a Risk Manager , Dr. Hassan Baba , said the recurrence of flood disasters reflects systemic negligence and poor governance . <p> He said , " In Nigeria , we do not value life , which is why we keep allowing floods and other disasters to repeat themselves . If we have a flood this year , and we know the cause , it should n't happen again next year for the same reason . <p> " The government response remains reactive rather than preventive . Instead of activating proactive measures , authorities prefer distributing relief materials to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ fail to reach victims . " <p> He cited poor drainage infrastructure as a critical factor in the country 's flood vulnerability . <p> " In many cities and towns , drainage systems are either poorly designed , insufficient for the volume of water during peak rains , or completely absent . Even where drains exist , they are frequently blocked by solid waste due to inadequate waste management and public awareness . This leads to water pooling on roads and in residential areas , turning streets into rivers during heavy downpours and increasing the risk of loss of lives and property damage . <p> " If reckless urbanisation is the cause , then urban and regional planners and any relevant authorities must take responsibility for approving such construction . " <p> Also , a lecturer in the Department Urban and Regional Planning , Federal Polytechnic , Nasarawa , Dr. Jetthro Makama , said flooding in the country is usually driven by a combination of factors , which include climate change , poor urban planning , and inadequate drainage systems . <p> He said , " Increased rainfall intensity and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ floods across many major cities . Similarly , rapid urbanisation without adequate infrastructure has left many areas vulnerable to flooding . <p> " It is no doubt that many cities lack proper drainage , leading to water accumulation during heavy rains . For instance , in 2024 , severe flooding affected over 8.5 million people across West and Central Africa , with Nigeria among the hardest hit . <p> " In rural areas , deforestation and logging worsen the problem by stripping away vegetation that naturally absorbs rainfall . The result is faster runoff , soil erosion , and flash floods that devastate communities . <p> " Without a shift toward proactive planning , environmental enforcement , and investment in resilient infrastructure , Nigeria will remain at the mercy of climate-induced disasters . " <p> Case study <p> Using Abuja , the nation 's capital , as a case study , an environmental activist , Christian Njoku , blamed blocked drainages and unregulated construction for incidence of flood in some part of Abuja . <p> " Abuja , Nigeria 's capital , was designed with modern infrastructure , including effective @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . However , recent years have seen a change across the Federal Capital Territory ( FCT ) . <p> " For instance , in September 2021 ; flooding in Trademore Estate resulted in fatalities and significant property damage . Similarly , over 100 houses in the Lugbe area were submerged following the June 2023 heavy rains , " he said . <p> Njoku also said , " And during the most recent in April 2025 , rain flooded the roads in Asokoro , making it difficult for cars and pedestrians to pass through . The roads looked like swimming pools , not well-constructed roads . <p> " Contributing factors to the recent flooding in the nation 's capital city were identified largely by blocked drainages and unregulated construction . Over the years , accumulation of waste in drainage channels impedes water flow ; while building on floodplains and waterways disrupts natural water courses , neglected infrastructure , the lack of maintenance has led to the deterioration of existing drainage systems . " <p> Solution <p> Speaking on the way out , the chairman of the Lagos State Urban Forest and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to implement sustainable policies , improve urban planning , and invest in early warning systems to safeguard vulnerable communities . <p> He said , " Nigeria has experienced severe and recurrent floods in recent years , largely due to climate change -- induced extreme weather events , poor urban planning , and inadequate drainage . Flooding is a growing concern . Climate change has intensified rainfall and raised sea levels , particularly in low-lying areas like Lagos , Bayelsa , and Delta states . <p> " There is an urgent need for climate adaptation strategies , including reforestation , protection of wetlands , enforcement of building codes , and disaster preparedness . " <p> He also recommended improved urban planning , relocation from flood-prone areas , and public education on sustainable environmental practices . <p> Similarly , the founder of the Rite-On-The-Beach Initiative , Akintunde Disu , said , " To mitigate flooding , Nigeria must enforce urban planning laws , prevent construction on flood-prone areas . There is also a need to invest in infrastructure , upgrade and maintain existing drainage systems . <p> " The government must also @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ by educating citizens on proper waste disposal to prevent drainage blockage . <p> " Also , the government implements early warning systems , by developing and utilising technology to forecast and respond to flood threats . " <p> He continued : " Flooding is not just an environmental problem , it is a sustainability issue . The way we manage our environment today directly affects the kind of world we leave for future generations . When we allow blocked drainages , cut down trees , or build recklessly on waterways , we create conditions that make flooding worse . But when we care for the environment , we build resilience . <p> " Sustainable development means meeting today 's needs without compromising the ability of future generations to meet theirs . Tackling flooding through proper waste management , responsible urban planning , and climate action is a key part of this . <p> If we want a Nigeria where our children can live safely and thrive , then protecting our environment must be a top priority . <p> " Flooding is no longer a distant concern ; it 's a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ government , communities , and individuals . Everyone has to be involved . This affects us all . Flooding does not just wash away homes , it washes away opportunities . Let 's build a Nigeria that lasts for us and those coming after us. " 
@@130485806 <p> As food demand surges and climate change worsens , the World Bank has urged Nigeria to invest heavily in irrigation farming , describing it as one of the most effective tools to boost yields , strengthen food security , and create jobs for millions of rural farmers . <p> In its latest report , " Scaling Solutions to Feed a Growing Population , " the Bank warned that the world 's population is projected to reach 10 billion by 2050 , driving food demand up by more than 50 percent . <p> " Smallholder farmers are on the frontlines of rising global demand , climate shocks , and water scarcity , " the report noted . " Irrigation , when developed within local water availability , can more than double yields while using less land and water per unit of output . " <p> In Nigeria , where most farmlands still depend on rainfall despite available water resources , the World Bank said scaling irrigation could transform rural livelihoods . <p> " Expanding irrigation infrastructure is not just about food -- it 's about jobs , stability , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ agricultural specialist with the World Bank . " With the right policies , Nigeria can feed its growing population and empower millions of farmers who currently struggle with unpredictable rains . " <p> Some farmers are already seeing the benefits . Mustapha Adamu , a smallholder farmer in Kano , said irrigation has changed his fortunes dramatically . <p> " The difference is clear , " Adamu said . " With irrigation , I no longer wait for the rains to come . My yields have doubled , and I can now plant twice a year . It 's given my family stability . " <p> Another farmer , Hajiya Ramatu , shared a similar experience : <p> " Before , we harvested once a year and sometimes lost everything to drought , " she said . " Now , even when the weather changes , we still have crops growing . It has brought us peace of mind . @@130503878 <h> Niyi Awodeyi : Gas , future to cleaner , cheaper energy sources <p> By : GREGORY AUSTIN NWAKUNOR <p> Date : 18 Oct 2025 <p> Share : <p> Niyi Awodeyi is an oil and gas expert . He has been involved in policy formulation for the oil and gas sector in Nigeria , working as a consultant in the pipelines and associated assets commercialisation drive , crude oil theft prevention committee and other associated initiatives geared towards improving the sector in Nigeria . He has also been involved in various conferences and seminars targeted at promoting and improving the Federal Government gas production , transportation and utilisation initiatives , working in consonance to support the Decade of Gas Initiative as led by the Minister of State for Petroleum ( Gas ) , Ekperike Ekpo and ably coordinated by the Director of the initiative , Mr Ed Ubong . He spoke to GREGORY AUSTIN NWAKUNOR . <p> Background I was born and brought @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is from Ondo while my mum is from Edo State , Benin , to be precise , from the Obaseki family . I went to the University of Ibadan , did my youth service programme in Warri , Delta State . I was initially posted to Bayelsa . We were the first set of Corps members posted to that state . But I redeployed to Delta , because I needed to serve in an oil and gas firm . The only oil and gas firm in Bayelsa then was Agip , it was in Brass Town . I was almost retained , but the intriguing thing about ethnicity made me lose that job . <p> Immediately after youth service , I was doing timber work . It was a programme that they would take graduates from Nigeria , train them to their standard , and then employ some of them . So we did that , and I got Shell employment to be a Shell staff . Days later , Schlumberger gave me employment to be an international staff . So I rejected Shell . <p> Was there any compensation to Shell for giving you that training ? No , it was n't compulsory for them to employ me . After the training , you would still go through the Shell interview . I did two and-a-half years in Equatorial Guinea . I was transferred back to Gabon as location manager to manage the business in Gabon . I did that for another two years . Then I was transferred to Abu Dhabi , United Arab Emirates . I was there for about a year . From Abu Dhabi , I was transferred to China . I was in China for another two and a half years . My whole career , I never worked in Nigeria for Schlumberger . It was only when I was about to leave Schlumberger in 2015 that I came back for just four months , and I left after the four months . Shortcrest and Subterra are into oil and gas services . <p> I and my company have been involved in promoting and actualising the Federal Government 's decade of gas initiative . Okay , maybe . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ importing do n't want them to work . Why would you want to import when you have refineries to do : Because they 're making money from importing . If I own bank shares and , two , three years , they do n't pay me dividend , I will sell off the shares and go and buy shares somewhere else that will pay me a dividend . That 's how NNPC should be . So you will now be forced to make a profit . How are you going to make a profit ? You have to work properly . You have to run the business like a business to make profit . That 's the rule . @@130503895 <h> Business , creativity , and AI ! These are all the highlights you need to know from the BRICS+ Fashion Summit 2025 <p> The BRICS+ Fashion Summit is a leading international platform that champions collaboration , innovation , and growth in emerging fashion markets . Packed with insights -- from cultural appropriation to brand positioning -- this year 's summit offered a deep dive into the future of global fashion . <p> Brazil , Russia , India , China , and South Africa are the core nations of the BRICS , an informal grouping of major emerging economies . Since its creation in 2009 , the BRICS has expanded to include more countries , such as Egypt , Ethiopia , Iran , and Indonesia . <p> The three-day global event took place at Moscow 's Zaryadye Concert Hall from August 28 to 30 , 2025 . It brought together designers , manufacturers , industry experts , entrepreneurs , and academic leaders from the core BRICS+ nations and more . <p> The Summit kicked off with a business program attended by representatives of government authorities , designers , and heads @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ include Anton Alikhanov , Minister of Industry and Trade of the Russian Federation ; Yang Jian , Executive Chairman of the China Fashion Association ; and Nana Tamakloe , Chief Executive Officer of Accra Fashion Week . <p> The business program featured 16 specialised sessions exploring investment opportunities in the fashion industry , modern technologies , and safeguarding cultural identities . Let 's go over our standout moments from the BRICS + Fashion summit . <h> Diversity in fashion should include disabilities <p> Panellist via Russian Fashion Council <p> Innovation was central , with discussions covering the integration of smart fabrics , biocompatible materials , AI , and big data . For years , fashion has been about exclusivity , but the 2020s have proven that there 's a demand for an inclusive industry . From Victoria 's Secret launching lingerie for pregnant people to SKIMS ' nude-coloured shapewear that comes in dark shades . However , the BRICS+ summit expanded on the current discussion of inclusivity , going beyond body diversity and including disabilities . <p> Yelena Rusakova , Director of the Innovative Design Lab , expressed , " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and the industry must start understanding their requests . Second , designers should include people with disabilities in fashion shows and adapt ready collections for them . " She discussed how manufacturers often overlook disabilities ; for example , raincoats are hardly wheelchair-accessible . <p> The market for clothing tailored to individuals with disabilities is experiencing rapid growth . In 2024 , the Adaptive Clothing Market size was valued at USD 1.6 billion and is projected to reach USD 4.8 billion by 2032 . It is not just an ethical issue , but one enabled by new technologies . Innovations include 3D body scanning , personalised design , AI , adaptive materials , and digital customisation . <h> Watch out for African fashion <p> Panellist via the Russian Fashion Council <p> The Regional Session on Africa highlighted the continent as a vast market with over 1.5 billion people and a unique cultural identity due to its more than 1,000 ethnic groups . Over a thousand ethnic groups contribute to a unique cultural code reflected in clothing design . But what strategies can help local African brands enter international markets ? @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ various African countries . <p> Anis Montacer , Founder &amp; CEO of Tunis Fashion Week and Sea Design Lab , emphasised that traditional embroidery inspires designers in Tunisia . Tunisian embroiderers not only decorate clothing but also preserve centuries-old craftsmanship that is gradually fading in the era of globalisation . <p> Egypt also has a fashion industry element to be proud of -- the famous Egyptian cotton . Susan Sabet , Board Member and Secretary General of the Egyptian Fashion &amp; Design Council , recalled its history : it began in the 1820s when the French experimented with long-staple Egyptian cotton . Today , the government is working to restore Egypt 's position as a leading supplier of cotton . <p> African fashion may be new to the international business scene , but with craftsmanship rooted in heritage and tradition , it is poised to make a global impact . <h> Global showcases of cultural heritage at the BRICS+ Fashion Summit <p> David Tlale runway look via Russian Fashion Council <p> Held alongside the business summit , the Moscow Fashion Week featured over 120 designers , and the grand International @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ craftsmanship of BRICS+ nations . <p> The Indian delegation , led by the Fashion Design Council of India , showcased homegrown textiles like woven cotton . Experts agreed that AI is emerging not as a competitor to designers , but as a collaborative tool . They affirmed that while AI excels at executing well-defined tasks , it can not replicate the human experience that drives authentic creativity , leading to the consensus that creative originality can evolve alongside AI . <p> Yu Chen , Founder and Design Director of Chen Yu Studio , expressed , " AI can make a huge number of designs , prints , and ornaments . Perhaps the future of fashion is a place where everyone feels seen .

It is implemented by the Lagos State Waterways Authority ( LASWA ) and will run from 2024 to 2030 . <p> It aims to develop 15 priority ferry routes . @@130504656 <h> Climate Change : Developments Rights in a just transition to Decarbonisation <p> Africa 's journey from a polluted environment to a sustainable and cleaner energy future must be guided by justice , equity , and accountability . Our climate and energy policies must protect the most vulnerable while empowering communities to thrive in a new economy . The continent can not simply repeat the exploitative , extractive models of industrialised nations ; to do so would be to sacrifice both development and dignity . <p> Although Africa contributes less than 4% of global carbon emissions , it bears the heaviest burden of their consequences . 162 million tons of man-made global warming pollutions emitted into the atmosphere every year and 8.7 Million people die every day to pollution and climate-related disasters . Yet , poorly designed or externally imposed " net zero " policies threaten to worsen these harms displacing communities , intensifying food insecurity , exploiting child labour , and eroding human rights . The real challenge is to achieve a just transition , one that is not only clean but also fair . <p> This requires coordinated @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ oversight ; the private sector must adopt responsible practices ; financial institutions must link funding to social and environmental outcomes ; and communities must be meaningfully involved in shaping their future . Integrating human and development -- what I often call EcoSoc ( Economic and Social ) -- rights into the green transition enables Africa to restore degraded environments , create livelihoods , and meet the Sustainable Development Goals . <h> From Environmental Neglect to Rights-Based Action <p> The relationship between environmental protection and human rights in Africa has evolved over time . After independence , the Organisation of African Unity ( OAU ) prioritised sovereignty and reconstruction , but environmental degradation soon revealed that progress without protection is hollow . The droughts and famines of the 1970s made it clear that a polluted environment undermines the very right to life and development . <p> The turning point came with the African Charter on Human and Peoples ' Rights ( 1981 ) , which for the first time enshrined the right to a " general satisfactory environment conducive to development . " Africa declared then that environmental care is intrinsic @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ began influencing the global conversation . At the 1992 Rio Earth Summit , African states championed the principle of common but differentiated responsibilities , insisting that those most responsible for pollution must bear the greatest responsibility for its correction . The UN Convention to Combat Desertification ( 1994 ) reinforced this , affirming that Africa 's struggles with drought and land degradation were inseparable from human rights and development . <p> By the early 2000s , the African Commission on Human and Peoples ' Rights , through Resolution 153 , recognised climate change as a direct human rights issue . Civil society movements , notably the Pan-African Climate Justice Alliance ( PACJA ) , strengthened this cause , reframing climate change from an environmental challenge into a demand for fairness , justice , and inclusion . <h> The African Charter and the Legal Foundation for a Fair Transition <p> The African Charter provides a powerful framework for balancing Africa 's developmental ambitions with its environmental responsibilities . <p> Article 21 recognises the sovereignty of African peoples over their natural resources . <p> Article 22 guarantees the right to equitable and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a clean and satisfactory environment . <p> Together , they define Africa 's normative guide for a just energy transition , one that reconciles the right to exploit resources with the duty to protect the environment . <p> This balance remains delicate . Many African economies still depend on oil , gas , and coal , yet these same industries have bred pollution , corruption , and rights abuses . Africa must therefore pursue cleaner energy pathways : solar , wind , hydro , and geothermal , without excluding the developmental rights of its people . The challenge is not the lack of vision , but of financing and institutional strength . Renewable energy in Africa costs several times more than in OECD countries , and our share of global climate finance remains unjustly low . <h> The Human Cost of Inaction <p> Despite numerous frameworks and declarations , the pace of progress is painfully slow . Climate inaction -- both globally and locally -- has turned Africa 's environmental crisis into a profound human and development rights emergency . <p> Across East Africa , prolonged droughts have left over @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ In Southern Africa , Cyclone Idai displaced nearly two million people and caused over a thousand deaths . In the west and central regions , floods , desertification , and shrinking lakes have sparked displacement and violent conflict . The Lake Chad Basin , which has lost over 90% of its water area since the 1960s , has become a symbol of how environmental degradation fuels insecurity . <p> These examples are not isolated tragedies ; they are evidence that environmental collapse strips people of their most basic rights ; to life , food , health , shelter , and dignity . The lesson is clear : climate change is not just an environmental issue ; it is the most urgent human rights challenge of our time . <h> Africa 's Development Paradox and Global Inequality <p> Africa 's development story remains marked by contradictions . We are rich in natural and human resources , yet poor in access and opportunity . Nearly 600 million Africans live without electricity . Meanwhile , global policies often demand that Africa decarbonise at the same pace as industrialised economies -- without recognising historical @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ end to fossil fuels disregard the fact that Africa still needs affordable energy to industrialise and lift millions out of poverty . Restricting development finance while limiting access to cleaner alternatives creates a form of climate colonialism -- a system that denies Africa the same developmental pathways once used by the West . <p> To counter this , Africa must centre human and development rights within its climate and energy agenda . Protecting vulnerable groups , enforcing environmental accountability , and ensuring fairness in global partnerships will strengthen both our domestic resilience and international negotiating power . <h> Global Commitments , Local Realities <p> The Paris Agreement ( 2015 ) reaffirmed principles that Africa had long advanced : that climate action must respect human rights , health , and development . Yet developed nations have failed to meet their obligations , particularly the promised $100 billion annual climate finance fund . <p> Nigeria 's experience reflects the continent 's broader challenge . As a signatory to the UNFCCC and the Paris Agreement , Nigeria pledged to cut emissions by 20% unconditionally and 47% conditionally by 2030 . Although the Climate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ did not enshrine binding targets , leaving investors uncertain and enforcement weak . Nigerian courts have also been reluctant to grant standing to civil society organisations on environmental issues , limiting accountability . Courts relying on a diminished non-justiciability provision of the constitution despite advancements in the Law and the bindingness of the Charter on Nigeria <h> Nigeria and the Paris Agreement <p> The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change ( UNFCCC ) ( 1992 ) was the precursor to both the Kyoto Protocol ( 1997 ) and the Paris Agreement ( 2015 ) . Nigeria is a signatory to the convention , along with 197 other signatory countries , as a non-annex 1 Party . Under the UNFCCC , under Article 4(2) ( a ) , each participating party is required to adopt national policies and corresponding measures for mitigating climate change . Subsumed under the UNFCCC 's broad negotiation platform , the Paris Agreement refines and strengthens the commitments of signatory states through their Nationally Determined Contributions ( NDCs ) . <p> According to Article 4(2) ( a ) of the United Nations Convention on Climate Change @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ adopt national policies and corresponding measures for mitigating climate change . <p> Unconditionally , Nigeria is committed to : <p> Working toward ending gas flaring by 2030 <p> Working toward off-grid solar PV of 13 GW ( 13,000 MW ) <p> Efficient gas generators <p> 2% per year energy efficiency ( 30% by 2030 ) <p> Transport shifts from car to bus <p> Improve the electricity grid <p> Climate-smart agriculture and reforestation <p> The conditional reductions imply that Nigeria will be dependent on assistance in funding and technical support from developed countries to fulfil 47% of its NDC commitment to reduce GHG by 2030 , under the Agreement . <p> Furthermore , since signing the Agreement in 2016 , the Nigerian Government passed the Climate Change Act in 2021 , providing the Agreement with access to the Nigerian legal system as a progressive step toward meeting its NDC targets . <p> Notwithstanding the enactment of the Climate Change Act 2021 , the Act does not contain the 20% unconditional and 47% conditional reduction in greenhouse gas ( GHG ) emissions that the country 's NDCs previously referred to . This @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ targets , provide certainty to investors , businesses and markets to promote investment in low-carbon technologies and sustainable practices . <p> Nigeria is still legally obligated to meet its 20% unconditional GHG reduction by 2030 NDC because it is covered by the principle of common but differentiated responsibilities ( CBDR ) in international environmental law , which states that all states are responsible for addressing global environmental destruction , yet not equally responsible . <p> Regarding the remaining 47% conditional reduction commitment , the Nigerian government is not legally obligated to meet it because the CBDR principle does not cover it , and it relies on financial support from developed countries , which Nigeria can not guarantee with certainty . <p> Considering the exclusion of the NDC 's from the Climate Change Act 2021 , enforcement mechanisms may prove inchoate because the 20% unconditional GHG target reduction can not be enforced against the government . <p> Article 4(9) of the Agreement requires Parties to communicate their NDCs every five years . On September 22 , 2025 , Nigeria submitted its latest Nationally Determined Contribution ( NDC 3.0 ) to the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the economy by 2060 despite the apparent lack of reform or support . <p> Regrettably , Nigerian courts have a track record of dismissing NGO 's from enforcing climate-related issues , such as Douglas v Shell , and the dismissal of Centre for Pollution Watch v Nigeria National Petroleum Corporation from the Court of Appeal because the NGO was said not to have suffered any damage or injury , as it filed the suit against the Defendant on behalf of a community affected by an oil spill . This narrow approach to legal interest ( locus standi ) is not helpful ; it is outdated and lacking in understanding of our global responsibility to the earth . <h> Moving Forward <p> To achieve a truly just transition , Africa must : <p> Implement human and development rights due diligence throughout the value chain . <p> Africa must build capacity ( skill-up ) and appoint representation that will negotiate and insist on international <p> collaboration and investment in sustainable development for emerging nations . <p> Take leadership in an astute Decarbonisation Legal framework , which is conspicuously lacking a legislative <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that is sensitive to development rights <p> Africa stands at a crossroads between vulnerability and resilience , dependence and self-determination . The continent 's future depends on how intentionally we act today . @@130533786 <h> Japan constructs world 's 1st wooden satellite <p> The world 's first wooden satellite has been built by Japanese researchers who said their tiny cuboid craft is scheduled to be carried into space off on a SpaceX rocket in September . <p> Each side of the experimental satellite developed by scientists at Kyoto University and logging company Sumitomo Forestry measures four inches . <p> The creators expect the wooden material will burn up completely when the device re-enters the atmosphere -- potentially providing a way to avoid the creation of metal particles when a retired satellite returns to Earth . <p> The metal particles could have a negative impact on the environment and telecommunications , the developers said as they announced the satellite 's completion on Tuesday . <p> " Satellites that are not made of metal should become mainstream , " Takao Doi , an astronaut and special professor at Kyoto University , told a press conference . <p> The developers plan to hand the satellite , made from magnolia wood and named LignoSat , to space agency JAXA next week . <p> It will be sent into @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in September , bound for the International Space Station ( ISS ) , they said . <p> From there , the satellite will be released from the Japanese ISS experiment module to test its strength and durability . <p> " Data will be sent from the satellite to researchers who can check for signs of strain and whether the satellite can withstand huge changes in temperature , " a Sumitomo Forestry spokeswoman told AFP on Wednesday . <p> Also on Tuesday , a rocket carrying a separate sophisticated satellite -- a collaboration between the European Space Agency ( ESA ) and JAXA -- blasted off from California on a mission to investigate the role clouds could play in the fight against climate change . <p> The EarthCARE satellite will orbit nearly 250 miles above Earth for three years .
