@@130840391 <h> These beautifully patterned creatures face extinction due to habitat loss , land clearance , the illegal pet trade and climate change . <p> Kenya 's exquisite little pancake tortoise , locally known as kobe kama chapati , faces extinction due to habitat loss , land clearance for agriculture , poaching for the illegal pet trade and potential impacts from climate change . <p> On 10 August , three suspects were arrested in a sting operation conducted by Kenya Wildlife Service ( KWS ) rangers and the police . The suspects were transporting four of these creatures to a buyer at the Marimanti trading centre in Tharaka-Nithi County . The sting operation came in the wake of KWS unveiling a National Recovery and Conservation Action Plan for the Pancake Tortoise ( Malacochersus tornieri ) in Kenya ( 2025-2035 ) on 16 May . <p> These unusually soft-shelled , beautifully patterned , relatively agile animals are endemic to Kenya 's arid and semi-arid landscapes , where they can be found in the crevices of rocky outcrops and hills . According to John Wambua , KWS assistant director in charge of the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Meru , Embu , Samburu , Laikipia , Isiolo , Kitui , Mwingi , Tana River and Marsabit counties . <h> Habitat of the pancake tortoise , Kenya <p> This tortoise species exhibits a low reproductive capacity , with females laying only one egg per clutch per season . Their recruitment rate ( the number of new tortoises added to the population , through birth , maturation , or migration ) is low . Survival of juveniles in the wild , especially given the numerous egg and juvenile predators present in the animal 's natural habitat , is also low . <p> The pancake tortoise is red-listed as critically endangered by the International Union for Conservation of Nature , and international trade is restricted under CITES ( Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora ) Appendix I. Despite this , tortoises are still poached and traded illegally in Kenya . <p> Protecting the species is complicated by two challenges . As the map shows , its habitat is widespread and includes community and private lands outside national parks and national reserves , making collaboration @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , national population size estimates , breeding , feeding , movements , home range , genetics and distribution are yet to be fully established to aid conservation planning . <p> Abraham Mugambi , a Marimanti community leader involved in the campaign to save the pancake tortoise , said a group of people from outside the area was responsible for paying desperate youths to poach the tortoises . The community intelligence system , he disclosed , had established that illicit dealers offered almost $100 for one animal . The community is , however , now on high alert . <p> Buyers smuggle the tortoises out of Kenya , primarily to Europe and North America , where the animal 's exotic characteristics make it a treasured pet in private homes and an addition to zoos . International wildlife trafficking networks also supply exotic meat and petmarketsin Thailand and elsewhere in Asia with endangered and threatened species , including the pancake tortoise . <p> To formulate a conservation strategy , KWS partnered with Turtle Survival Alliance through the Lewa Wildlife Conservancy , National Museums of Kenya and Northern Rangelands Trust to establish the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ years , the committee conducted a baseline study ( using data available in the public domain and from the literature ) , stakeholder mapping and consultations at multiple sites , as well as community and technical validations . This culminated in the 2025-2035 action plan launched by the Tourism and Wildlife Ministry on 16 May . <p> The planaimsto protect and restore vital koppie habitats , train local ' tortoise guardians ' to lead conservation efforts , target wildlife trafficking networks , boost scientific research and population tracking , and create eco-tourism opportunities to generate local income . <p> " Our young men , elders and women have been taught about the threats the species face and have been informed to alert the chief or elders in case they see any stranger or anyone looking for the tortoises , " Mr Mugambi said . <p> Speaking at the launch , KWS Director General Erustus Kanga called on county governments in regions where the tortoise is found to designate protected habitat zones . He asked them to allocate funding for public awareness campaigns and establish county-level wildlife monitoring and enforcement units @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is n't just about saving a tortoise , " he said . " It 's about protecting a living emblem of Kenya 's natural heritage -- a species that defies nature 's norms yet now depends on our protection . With females laying just one egg per year , every individual is critical to the species ' survival . " <p> Peter Nyawira of Lewa Wildlife Conservancyadded : " We 're not just launching a document . We 're building a coalition -- counties , communities , scientists and policymakers -- for one small tortoise that tells a bigger story about our disappearing drylands . " <p> The efforts by KWS and its partners will hopefully usher in a decade of evidence-driven scientific and community understanding of the pancake tortoise and its habitat . The plan should also establish collaborative conservation interventions that work with communities and curb the poaching and illicit trade of the tortoise . <p> The stakeholders involved in implementing the ambitious 10-year plan should raise public awareness on the ecological and tourist value of this unique tortoise . They should mobilise resources from international conservation bodies @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ enforcement agencies need to collaborate with KWS , communities and private landowners to curb the illicit trade in pancake tortoises . Community-embedded intelligence gathering and the speedy prosecution of arrested poachers would boost such efforts .

@@130840638 <h> Forest is life -- but Nigeria 's last rainforest is falling fast <p> Felled trees , cut off from their roots in a deforested part of the Agoi-Ibami forest in Cross Rive state , Nigeria , July 21 , 2025 . Photograph Akintunde Akinleye/CCIJ <p> BY UCHENNA IGWE <p> Thick clouds gathered over Okwabang , Cross River statein Southern Nigeria . The skies rumbled , letting down the rain that barely lasted 15 minutes . Patrick Obi sighed , unimpressed by the meagre offering that had eclipsed his hopes of a heavy downpour . It was n't always like this , he says , recalling memories of intense rainfall from the years before . <p> " When I was tender younger , around this time , I believe we hardly got clear skies because it was always raining . Over time now , we have not had that intense rain . The weather used to be very cold before , but now there are days we feel the heat as if we are in the northern part of the country , " he said . " People have to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ will open their windows for ventilation . " <p> Obi described the situation as " the aftermath of deforestation " in his community , a view supported by the World Resources Institute ( WRI ) , a global agency focused on environmental protection . <p> Advertisement <p> " Removal of forest cover , especially in the tropics , increases local temperatures and disrupts rainfall patterns in ways that compound the local effects of global climate change , threatening severe consequences for human health and agricultural productivity , " the WRI notesin its 2022reporton the effects of deforestation on climate . <p> A biodiversity belt that once stretched across the country 's south , Patrick 's Cross River State holds much of what is now left of Nigeria 's tropical rainforest , nestled within a national park and 13 forest reserves . It is home to a vast number of lucrative tree species , including mahogany , ebony , opepe , apa , bomba and African bush mango , which is known locally as ogbono . However , these forests are fast disappearing due to massive logging operations by businesses and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , the state lost more than 540 square miles of its tree cover , about the size of the city of Los Angeles , according to Global Forest Watch , which uses satellite data to monitor the world 's forests . The loss is due to illegal and unregulated logging operations across the state by businesses , groups and individuals who have besieged the communities with chainsaws , thinning out the forests and threatening biodiversity . <p> Advertisement <p> Logging in Cross River communities is as old as the forests themselves . Here , many families rely on the forest economy for food , medicine , firewood , housing and other essential uses . <p> " We survive on economic trees like cocoa , ogbono trees and the rest . That is the only source of our income . It is what we use to feed and train our children , " Chief Genesis Aria of Butatong community in Boki local government area told the Centre for Collaborative Investigative Journalism ( CCIJ ) . <p> A labourer holding a chainsaw cuts through a log of wood at the edge of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 2025 . While efforts by some local conservationists are geared towards preserving the forest at Agoi-Ibami , the rate of depletion of the rainforest by local wood contractors in the town is alarming . Photograph Akintunde Akinleye/CCIJ <p> But reporting by CCIJ found that loggers are ignoring government efforts to regulate deforestation . Critics charge that government enforcement is lax . Gold mining is also destroying parts of the rainforest . The extensive destruction of forests is not only fueled by economic pressures and poor oversight , but community leaders are also spreading false claims about how cutting down trees will benefit the region . This includes disinformation that fewer trees will reduce the incidence of malaria , even though the scientific consensus documents that the loss of rainforest significantly increases the risk of disease . <h> From logging bans to illicit timber <p> The rich soil in these forests makes for land much sought after by farmers to plant cash crops like the African bush mango , pineapples , oil palm and cocoa , a key ingredient in chocolate that has increased in value , fueling new waves of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ lands for large-scale agriculture grows , farmers have been encroaching deeper into forest reserves . Photograph Akintunde Akinleye/CCIJ <p> In 2022 , environmental groups alleged Dansa Agro , Wilmar International and 20 other agribusinesses engaged in illegal logging within Cross River 's protected forests , disguising their work as salvaging land for farming . Efforts by successive governments in Cross River State to secure the forests have failed on the back of government complicity and lax enforcement . The area gained only a fifth of an acre of new growth within the same period . This is not exclusively due to the massive plundering of forest trees by loggers and farmers , but also due to gold mining deep into the forest . <p> The recent discovery of gold in communities like Agoi Ibami and Agoi Ekpo has drawn the influx of miners from across the country , including foreigners from neighbouring Niger . To access gold deposits buried beneath the ground , the miners have cleared large swathes of trees and excavated the soil with the use of bulldozers . <p> To extract gold , artisanal gold miners often use cyanide and mercury , two poisonous materials with degrading impacts on the environment and toxic effects on humans and animals , even at very low levels of exposure . In Zamfara , Northern Nigeria , lead poisoning from gold mining led to the death of at least 400 children in 2010 , with many more exposed to severe health risks . <p> There are also concerns over security threats @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in the communities . In January , the Attah , a traditional ruler of Agoi Ekpo , Michael Onun Mbang , raised security concerns over foreigners illegally mining gold in the communities ' forests . This led the State 's Security Adviser , Major General Ubi-Obono , to order the illegal miners to vacate the forests . Multiple locals who spoke to CCIJ confirmed that many of the foreigners were still present in the forests . <h> Rich in resources but living in hardship <p> Despite being a vast reservoir of forestry , gold and other rich natural resources , Cross River communities suffer neglect and lack social amenities that would improve life for their population . The roads are hardly drivable due to the movement of heavy machinery and the busy traffic of heavy trucks transporting timber out of the communities . There are no public water works and no electricity . For instance , the single road leading into the Agoi community through neighbouring Mkpani , has been dilapidated for years , making it impassable for vehicles , especially during the rainy season . <p> A woman squeezes @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ extract palm oil into a bowl at an oil palm mill at the edge of Agoi-Ibami forest in Agoi-Ibami town in Cross River , Nigeria , July 21 , 2025 . People in the region say farm clearing by local farmers and forest depletion by local and foreign wood contractors contribute to the thinness of the forest in recent years . Photograph Akintunde Akinleye/CCIJ <p> " Look at this place , does it look like a place that produces all these resources ? You saw the state of our roads on your way here , " community leader Etim Aitu said . <p> The same reality occurred many kilometres away in Okwabang , Ekuri , Ochon , Ekori , amongst other forest communities that CCIJ visited . Most of these communities have been in darkness for over a decade , relying largely on generators and solar power owned by a privileged few . For instance , multiple residents in Ochon and Ekori confirmed that their communities last saw electricity in 2014 . <h> Logging fueled by disinformation <p> Beyond poverty and other economic reasons , the devastation of Nigeria 's @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ communities . <p> One of such narratives claims that the forests constitute a health risk to residents , breeding mosquitoes and contributing to malaria in the communities . Community chief Genesis Aria is one of those who consider the forest complicit in the spread of malaria , having lost his mother to the disease as a child . <p> " As of 1996 , when I was growing up , I discovered that our people were dying of malaria . My mother was also affected ; she had severe malaria , and we lost her , " he told CCIJ . Before now , they ( the government ) were giving us mosquito nets to protect us from mosquitoes , but for like 6 years now , they have stopped bringing those mosquito nets . Reports and scientific studies reviewed by CCIJ identified deforestation as a significant way that humans contribute to the spread of malaria , accounting for a 5% increase in cases among children in rural Nigeria . <p> Several studies detailed how deforestation increases the risk of the disease , emphasising that nations with higher rates of deforestation also tend to have higher rates of malaria . <p> The result , according to a University of Floridaanalysis of 17 scientific studies , is that slightly more than half of mosquitoes in deforested habitats were confirmed to be carrying malaria , more than twice as many as those in forested areas . <p> In Yakurr and Boki LGAs , CCIJ observed that the forested areas where illegal loggers cut trees are located at least three to four miles ( 5-6 km ) away from human settlements . This long distance naturally provides a buffer zone between the forests and settlements , which significantly reduces the risk of malaria transmission , as disease-borne @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ from their habitats . <h> Another factor affecting mosquitoes is rising temperatures . <p> Thermal data obtained by CCIJ from these locations -- Agoi Ibami and Inyang Iba ( Yakurr LGA ) -- in Cross Rivers State show temperatures on the ground increased in places where trees were cleared , there was less shade , and the soil was exposed to more sunlight . Temperatures increased as much as 9 degrees Fahrenheit ( +5 ? ) . Higher temperatures may contribute to malaria-carrying Anopheles gambiaebecoming more able to transmit disease , according to studies that have appeared in The Lancet , Nature and other scientificpublications . <p> That is not the only disinformation spread about cutting down rainforests . <p> In some quarters , it is believed that the forests exist eternally and , as such , will continue to regrow and replenish no matter how many trees are cut down . <p> Collins Okwor , a resident also in the logging business , likens forest life to the nature of the evolving human population . Timber is just like the human race , he said as he enjoyed a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ more are born , and the younger ones grow . In the forest , we have young trees ; when the older ones are cut off , the younger ones will surely grow up , " he said . <p> His opponent , Collins Obi nodded in agreement , his gaze still on his cards , seemingly plotting his next move . " We can not exhaust the timber in the forest . The more you cut , the more others grow . So , it is impossible for them to be exhausted , " he said . <p> The science is less clear . It can take years for a rainforest to regrow . The projections vary depending on whether scientists are looking for positive signs , like biodiversity and soil recovery , to the forest returning to how it was before . It might take from a couple of decades for the first stages of recovery to more than a century when trees and animals might fully return . <p> Others argue that the loggers are rendering a sort of social service to the communities by using their @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that have helped farmers access their farms easily . <p> Sylvester Amah , a cocoa farmer living in Boki local government area , said the government 's neglect and failure to provide social services to the community has caused them to rely on loggers for access roads.Repeated requests for comment from CCIJ to the Cross River Forestry Commission , even through the state 's chapter of the Nigerian Environmental Society , were not responded to . <p> Already ravaged by desertification in the North , the rapid disappearance of Nigeria 's remaining rainforest in the South due to the ongoing massive deforestation spells a looming environmental crisis that threatens critical ecosystems and intensifies the country 's climate challenges . <p> Odey Oyama said illegal logging in Cross River persists due to the government 's neglect of environmental conservation and lax enforcement by its agencies . " The Forestry Commission is not working . None of the agencies responsible for this issue is working . The government just stays in Calabar and makes funny pronouncements . There is nothing like environmental protection in the state , " he told CCIJ . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ been one of the critical voices leading advocacy for the preservation of the state 's forests with his Rainforest Resource and Development Centre . " <p> He identified the pillars as coordinated action among security agencies under a unified State Security Council chaired by him , the creation of Community Protection Guards ( CPG ) to support law enforcement operations , and an intelligence-sharing network linking traditional rulers , local councils , and security units . <p> Advertisement <p> Lawal said his administration also set up peace committees in every local government area to mediate disputes and restore public trust . <p> According to him , Zamfara was working with the Office of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to implement Preventing and Countering Violent Extremism ( PCVE ) initiatives that focus on rehabilitation , reintegration , and trauma healing . <p> The governor expressed concern about recent peace arrangements being pursued independently by local actors in parts of the North-West , warning that such informal negotiations with armed groups risk deepening insecurity . <p> " Dialogue can aid conflict management , but true peace requires disarmament . Reconciliation without control equates to surrender . Allowing armed groups to keep their weapons while setting peace terms simply delays violence and compromises State sovereignty , " he warned . <p> He insisted that peace processes must be led by state authorities with full legitimacy , stressing that stability depends on government control , credible enforcement , and a demonstrated commitment to justice . <p> Advertisement <p> " Sustainable peace arises when armed actors acknowledge the rule of law and the Government 's commitment to protect its citizens , " Lawal concluded . <p> Commandant of the National Institute for Security Studies , Mr J.O . @@130879927 <h> PCNGi : Can youth leader become climate leader ? <p> The Presidential CNG Initiative is a flagship programme of the Federal Government of Nigeria , launched in 2023 to promote the widespread adoption of compressed natural gas and electric vehicles , especially in mass transit systems , to drive down the cost of transportation , create jobs , and engender energy security and environmental sustainability in the country . Compressed natural gas , which is methane stored at high pressure for use in vehicles and some stationary engines , is a cleaner alternative to petrol and diesel . It reduces local air pollution and lowers the carbon dioxide produced per kilometre , which means that it helps to fight climate change . <p> When , three months ago , President Bola Tinubu appointed Barrister Ismaeel Ahmed as the executive chairman of the PCNGi , I , for one , was excited , because one can never overemphasise the fundamental importance of leadership in climate governance . Every successful project -- from education reform to green economy initiatives -- tells a human story . It reflects how one person 's @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ shared goal . In truth , systems are only as strong as the people running them . Institutions are frameworks ; personalities animate them . <p> In fact , the personality factor is a reality in all spheres of governance . Policies do not implement themselves . Institutions do not move on their own . Projects do not succeed merely because they are well-written on paper . It is the person behind them -- their commitment , credibility , charism , and capacity -- that determines the true outcome . A visionary leader provides direction , seeing beyond immediate gains to long-term impact . But vision alone is not enough ; credibility sustains it . When citizens trust the person behind a policy , they are more likely to buy into the process , comply with reforms , and even defend the project during turbulent times . <p> To be sure , successful social or environmental reforms across the world often bear the imprint of one resolute individual whose conviction inspired collective action . Whether it is a national electrification programme or a climate adaptation strategy , it is the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of a project driver influences how they manage people , resources , and crises . A collaborative leader encourages inclusiveness and shared ownership , ensuring that stakeholders feel invested . Policies may be written in offices , but they are brought to life by the passion , credibility , and purpose of the people behind them . So , when personality aligns with vision and integrity , governance becomes not just a function but a force for transformation . <p> The present Nigerian society needs this leadership injection . As citizens who have converted their vehicles to CNG now complain bitterly about the scarcity of the gas , there should be a trusted voice of reassurance -- that the project shall work . Citizens must believe that policies like the CNG initiative are not merely another government programme but a promise kept to protect both livelihoods and the planet . <p> Every policy meets resistance . The CNG initiative faces hurdles -- infrastructure gaps , public scepticism , funding delays , and safety concerns . Yet , the personal traits of resilience , empathy , and adaptability within the leadership @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Nigeria 's energy transition story is still being written . Whether the PCNGi becomes a lasting legacy , or a fleeting experiment , will depend less on paperwork and more on personality -- the determination , integrity , and credibility of those driving it . <p> To me , the CNG initiative is more than a technical policy ; it is a statement of intent . It seeks to move Nigeria away from petrol and diesel dependency toward cleaner , cheaper , and more sustainable energy alternatives . But the project 's success , like every ambitious reform before it , will ultimately depend not on the elegance of its design , but on the personality and leadership behind its implementation . <p> This is where President Tinubu got it right . If he had left it to sail with the wind , it would have eventually been sucked into a whirlpool of ennui and social despondency . But his move to signpost a leader has great potential . Ismaeel Ahmed has something going for him . He is a youth leader , and a youth leader is a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ previous life as a media assistant for the defunct Congress for Progressive Change . That was the time when being a youth leader in an opposition party was not something to wish for . But some people took up the gauntlet , even when there was an opportunity to sell out and join the ruling party for largesse or play a double agent game of living like a chameleon -- opposition by day and ruling party by night . Ahmed was one of such young Nigerians . Freshly minted from a leading UK university , he strutted all over the north working with the youth mobilisation arm of the CPC . <p> Indubitably , it was a test of the stuff some citizens were made of , especially those who joined the CPC because of Muhammadu Buhari 's green credentials . Yours truly was one of those who joined because of the late General 's climate ideals , and I have no regrets . <p> Ahmed 's other credential is his single-mindedness in pursuing a vision . Popularly known as ' Leader ' , Ahmed was sold on the idea @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ APC . In June 2021 , he organised the maiden edition of the APC Youth Summit , which was aimed at deepening the democratic process through youth inclusion . With the inaugural event headlining high-level government participation -- with Vice President Osinbajo chairing -- the youth summit has become an institution that outlived its creator . I hope Ahmed injects such creative dedication into the PCGNi . <p> Thirdly , having served as the Senior Special Assistant on National Social Investment Programme to former President Buhari from 2018 to 2022 , the lawyer has undoubtedly absorbed the ambient mind-frame capable of enlarging the PCNGi into grassroots empowerment . <p> For instance , he could incorporate the Carbon Credit Trading Scheme , adopted by the Paris Climate Agreement in 2023 , as a major tributary of the project in the country 's efforts to combat climate change . To be specific , Bio-CNG is an important project category eligible for carbon credits . Carbon credits are financial instruments designed to mitigate greenhouse gas emissions and address climate change . <p> Generally , one carbon credit represents the reduction , removal , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ its equivalent in greenhouse gas emissions . The Clean Development Mechanism , established under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change 's Kyoto Protocol in 1997 , enables countries to earn carbon credits by investing in emission reduction initiatives in developing countries . <p> Among the various pathways to earn carbon credits , the production of Bio-CNG is one . Bio-CNG is a sustainable fuel derived from biogas purification , offering a renewable alternative to non-renewable CNG sources . It shares the same properties as CNG and can be used as fuel for vehicles , electricity generation and industrial purposes . Biogas is generated through the anaerobic decomposition of organic materials , including municipal solid waste , agricultural residue , wastewater and press mud . The carbon credits earned from producing a tonne of Bio-CNG fall within the range of 16 to 25 credits , depending on the feedstock utilised . Each credit holds a value between $5 and $15 in the current international market . <p> Ultimately , climate policy such as CNG rollout demands not only a vision for sustainability but also the credibility to rally diverse @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and local technicians . The Nigerian public , weary of promises that never materialise , needs to see and trust the human face behind the reform . It is about people , those who design , drive , and defend the policy at every stage . Climate action , especially in developing nations , is never a straight road . Nigeria , being a regional leader , must position itself to guide others through this labyrinth of green hope . <p> All rights reserved . @@130919096 <h> Nigeria and the dangers of overpopulation <p> 31st October 2025 <p> By Ladi Ayodeji <p> Nigeria stands at the edge of a demographic precipice . The country is hurtling toward a population explosion that could define its destiny for better or worse . Projections suggest that , by 2050 , Nigeria will rank as the world 's fourth most populous nation , behind only China , India and Indonesia . With current estimates ranging between 200 and 250 million people , the country 's growth rate shows no signs of restraint . Yet , there seems to be little urgency in addressing what could become one of Nigeria 's most formidable challenges . <p> The warning signs are already visible . Food insecurity is deepening , while climate change intensifies droughts , floods , and other extreme conditions that disrupt fragile ecosystems . The balance between human needs and available resources is tilting dangerously . Without deliberate intervention , Nigeria risks descending into a cycle of scarcity and social unrest . <p> Efforts at population control have historically been inconsistent . The first major attempt came during the Babangida @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ impact remains debatable , it at least acknowledged the issue and sparked national awareness . Unfortunately , that awareness has since faded . Successive governments have failed to sustain meaningful population education or implement modern family planning programs at scale . <p> Other nations once faced similar demographic crises but acted decisively . China 's one-child policy , though controversial , curbed its explosive growth long enough for economic transformation to take root . India , despite its vast numbers , has managed to balance population and production through technological advancement , agricultural innovation , and sustained investment in human capital . Both countries have leveraged their populations as assets , not liabilities . <p> Nigeria , however , remains trapped in a cycle of underdevelopment . Agriculture , the sector that should guarantee food security , still operates largely at a subsistence level . Despite vast stretches of arable land , food imports continue to rise while farmlands disappear under the weight of unchecked urban sprawl and speculative real estate projects . The result is a paradox : a nation with abundant natural resources yet unable to feed itself @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ confront this reality , but it must be handled with integrity . Past censuses have been marred by ethnic rivalries , religious politics , and manipulation for political advantage . For once , the exercise must prioritize accuracy over politics . Reliable data is not just about numbers ; it is the foundation for planning , resource allocation , and policy formulation . Without it , national development will continue to drift blindly . <h> Related News <p> A realistic understanding of Nigeria 's demographics is essential for tackling its complex challenges , from unemployment and insecurity to poor healthcare and inadequate housing . Today , millions of young Nigerians face joblessness , pushing many into crime , cyber fraud , drug abuse , or extremist movements . These are not isolated social problems ; they are symptoms of uncontrolled population growth and weak economic planning . <p> Comparatively , nations with fewer natural resources have achieved far more through discipline and foresight . The Gulf states , for example , invested oil revenues into education , infrastructure , and diversification . Nigeria , endowed with even greater potential , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ greed . Instead of channeling oil wealth into productivity and innovation , leaders used it to finance patronage networks , inflated contracts , and endless cycles of waste . <p> The structure of governance itself has become part of the problem . Nigeria 's political system is excessively expensive and riddled with competition that rewards division over development . Elections are often zero-sum contests , driven by ethnic and religious sentiments rather than competence or vision . The pursuit of power has become a means of personal enrichment rather than national advancement . <p> What Nigeria needs now is not another round of rhetoric , but a comprehensive rethinking of its social , economic , and political priorities . Population control must be treated as a development imperative , not a taboo . Family planning , women 's education , and rural empowerment must form the core of national policy . Technology and innovation should drive agricultural reform and job creation . Governance must shift from extraction to accountability . <p> Nigeria 's greatest resource is its people , but only if they are healthy , educated , and productively @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ rather than a blessing . The time to act is now , before the numbers overwhelm the nation 's capacity to provide for its citizens . The path forward requires courage , discipline , and a collective recognition that unchecked growth without development is not progress ; it is a slow-motion disaster . <p> * Ayodeji is an author , speaker , counselor . He can be reached on 09059243004 ( SMS only )

@@130919543 <p> President Bola Tinubu has approved the adoption of a national carbon market framework , and the operationalisation of the climate change fund . <p> The carbon market framework provides the rules and structure for Nigeria to trade carbon credits -- a permit that allows a country sell their greenhouse gas emissions reduction to others that need help meeting their climate targets . <p> The president also restored the National Council on Climate Change ( NCCC ) to the national budget to enable it receive FAAC allocation . <p> In a statement on Monday signed by Stanley Nkwocha , senior special assistant to the President on media , Tinubu said the approvals aim to position Nigeria to tap into the global carbon market and attract up to $3 billion annually in carbon finance over the next decade . <p> The president , who granted the approvals after a presentation by Omotenioye Majekodunmi , NCCC director-general , at the council 's meeting on Thursday in Abuja , said climate action remains central to his administration 's agenda . <p> Tinubu , who was represented by Kashim Shettima , the vice-president , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ international carbon market and accelerate the country 's climate actions . <p> Advertisement <p> " Addressing climate change is not just an environmental imperative but also an opportunity to unlock new investments , jobs , and innovations across the energy , agriculture , and industrial sectors , " Tinubu said . <p> " Nigeria stands ready to take its rightful place as a global leader in climate action , ensuring that our voice and reality are heard and respected in international negotiations . " <p> He said his administration 's focus ahead of the 30th United Nations Climate Change Conference ( COP30 ) , billed to hold in Bel ? m , Brazil , is to harness financing opportunities for climate-resilient projects and related interventions . <p> " We will continue to champion policies that protect our people , strengthen our economy , and position Nigeria as a destination for green investment and innovation , " he said . <p> Advertisement <p> Earlier , Majekodunmi said the meeting was timely as it would shape Nigeria 's global climate positioning ahead of COP30 . <p> She said the council secretariat is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's climate goals into measurable outcomes . <p> Presenting the council 's progress report , she noted that Nigeria is now eligible to access new rounds of climate finance from multilateral funds . <p> Majekodunmi said the council 's key requests include adopting the national carbon market framework , operationalising the Climate Change Fund , and restoring the NCCC 's budget line within the annual FAAC allocation to ensure financial stability . <p> Advertisement <p> Wale Edun , minister of finance and coordinating minister of the economy , endorsed the recommendations , saying Nigeria must secure a strong position within the global carbon framework . <p> He pledged the finance ministry 's support , including plans to coordinate a quarterly climate finance tracking dashboard .
